Let's think of the optimization landscape. The objective is max of many bilinear forms. A coordinate-wise optimum can be improved by moving multiple coordinates jointly. The exact CD solves each coordinate with only linear equality? It skips coordinates at 0/1. In solutions, many coordinates may be 0/1. If stuck with many at boundaries, coordinate descent with single variables cannot adjust global constraint if sum changes? It updates one coordinate and then projects sum (shifts all variables to restore). Projection can perturb many and destroy coordinate optimality but helps. Maybe a better approach uses feasible directions preserving sum: when changing coordinate p, adjust another coordinate q to maintain sum without global projection. The current algorithm changes p freely then projects by scaling all h. This may introduce slow drift. We can implement pair coordinate exact for two variables with sum fixed: update (h_p,h_q) to x, s-x preserving total, exactly minimizing max over 1D? That could improve and avoid projection. For binary-like inside, many coordinates are 0/1; pair moves inside (0,1) of two variables may allow moving mass from one to another. Exact pair minimax: objective terms: for each lag m, c_m is quadratic in x and y: terms involving p and q:
- diagonal: -x^2 - y^2 from m=0? and c_0 terms.
- edge between p and q: if q = p+d, terms c_d: x(1-y), c_-d: y(1-x) contribute -x - y + 2xy? Let's derive. Other terms linear in x and y weighted by outside variables. So as function f(x,y) = Q xy + linear x + linear y - x^2 - y^2? Since xy coefficient positive? c includes x(1-y) + y(1-x) - x^2 -y^2? x -xy + y -xy -x^2 -y^2 = x+y-2xy -x^2-y^2. Bilinear coefficient -2. The max of such nonconvex? We can solve via优化 in square [0,1], x+y=s (if we restrict sum fixed). Then y=s-x, one-dimensional (same as coordinate) but can move mass between two; maybe equivalent to coordinate variable s? `swap_update(c, h, i, j, n)`:
Assume h is np.uint8 or int8 array 0/1, c int32 length 2n-1. We will swap i,j with different values.
Implementation with vectorized slices:
```
def flip_update(c, h, p, delta, n):
    # delta = new - old, ±1
    right_len = n - p - 1
    if right_len > 0:
        c[n : n + right_len] += delta * (1 - h[p+1:])
        c[n-2 : p-1 : -1] -= delta * h[p+1:]
    left_len = p
    if left_len > 0:
        c[n+left_len-1 : n-1 : -1] -= delta * h[:p]
        c[:left_len] += delta * (1 - h[:p])
```
Need check slice lengths and direction:
Right q=p+1..n-1 length L.
For q>p:
 c[n:n+L] corresponds d=1..L. h[p+1:] order q increasing, d increasing. `c[n:n+L] += delta*(1-h[p+1:])` good.
 c[n-2:p-1:-1] start n-2, step -1 down to p inclusive? stop p-1 exclusive => last index p. length n-2 - p +1 = n-p-1=L. h[p+1:] d increasing as q increasing; for q=p+1 d=1 idx n-2; q=n-1 d=L idx p. So reversal matches: c[n-2], h[p+1]; c[n-3],h[p+2]; good.
Left q=0..p-1 length L=p.
 d=p-q: q=0 d=p idx n-1+p; q=p-1 d=1 idx n. We want c indices n+p-1 down to n. Slice c[n+L-1 : n-1 : -1] start n+L-1, stop n-1 exclusive => end n. length L. h[:p] order q=0..p-1, d decreasing; matches: c[n+p-1] with h[0], c[n+p-2] with h[1], ..., c[n] with h[p-1]. We subtract delta*h[q] for pair(q,p): yes.
 Negative idx: offset p-q? pair(p,q) negative offset -(d), index n-1-d = q. Slice c[:L] with h[:p] order q=0..p-1: add delta*(1-h[q]). good.
Diagonal c[p]? offset0 unchanged for binary. no.
```
def swap_binary(c,h,i,j,n):
    # assume h[i] != h[j]
    # swap by two flips: first flip i, then j, using current h arrays.
    hi = int(h[i]); hj = int(h[j])
    delta_i = hj - hi  # for i, new value hj; if hi=1,hj=0 delta=-1
    delta_j = hi - hj  # for j, new value hi; +1
    flip_update(c, h, i, delta_i, n)
    h[i] = hj
    flip_update(c, h, j, delta_j, n)
    h[j] = hi
```
Need ensure flip_update uses h values for q≠p. For first flip at i, h[j] is old hj=0 if swapping 1->0. After first flip, h[i]=0. Then second flip at j (old 0 ->1), h[j] still 0; flip_update uses h[i]=0 (updated), and other values. The change from old state to new state should include pair (i,j) contributions twice? Let's verify. Old state has i=1,j=0. After first flip i to 0, c updated to state A (i=0,j=0). After second flip j to1, c to state B (i=0,j=1). Total change = old->B. Good. flip_update formula assumes flipping p from old to old+delta with all other h as current (including the other flip updated). Works.

Potential issue: `1 - h[p+1:]` where h uint8 gives uint8? If multiplied by delta (int -1), result signed? `delta * (1 - h)` if h uint8: 1 - h uint8? For h=1, 0; for 0, 1. Multiplying Python int -1 by uint8 array yields int64? Okay. But to be safe, store h as int8 or float? h int8; 1-h int8; negative multiplication okay. Use h = np.array(h,dtype=np.int8). c int64 maybe. Update c += delta * ... where RHS int8, delta int -> int64. Good.

`c.max()` each swap on int64 array length 1400. Okay.

def binary_anneal(h,n,steps,temp_bounds,deadline,rng,target_count=None):
  h = h.astype(int8).copy()
  c = np.correlate(h, 1-h, 'full').astype(int64)
  cur = int(c.max())
  best_h=h.copy(); best_c=cur
  # temperature in counts
  t_start = max(3, int(cur - best_target?)) maybe 5
  t_end = 0
  for it in range(steps):
    if time > deadline-break
    # choose i,j
    for tries in 20:
      i=int(rng.randint(n)); j=int(rng.randint(n)); if i!=j and h[i]!=h[j]: break
    if invalid continue
    temp = t_start*(1 - p) + t_end*p maybe acceptance allow cur+? Since cur integer, accept if new <= cur + ceil(temp)
    old_h = h.copy()? Revert by swap again; but we need newmax before accept. We can call swap_binary, new=c.max(); if new <= best? update best. Accept if new <= cur + accept_int.
    if accept: cur = int(new); if cur<best: best=...
    else: swap_binary(c,h,i,j,n)
```

```

       top = np.argpartition(c, -min(5,len(c)))[-5:]
       idx = top[rng.randint(len(top))]

       elif k<0:
          d=-k; arr = np.nonzero((h[d:]==1)&(h[:-d]==0))[0]+d
       else: arr empty
       if len(arr)>0:
          i = int(arr[rng.randint(len(arr))])
          jpos = rng.randint(len(zeros_list)); j=int(zeros_list[jpos])
       else: choose random from lists
    else: oi=rng.randint(len(ones_list)); i=int(ones_list[oi]); jpos=rng.randint(len(zeros_list)); j=int(zeros_list[jpos])
    # perform swap
    swap_binary(c,h,i,j,n)
    new = int(c.max())
    if new <= cur + allow:
       cur = new
       update lists
       if new < best: best_h=h.copy(); best=new
    else:
       swap_binary(c,h,i,j,n) # revert
       # lists unchanged
  # after phase maybe if no improvement, lower allow
return best_h,best_count
```

def slsqp_polish(h,n,taus,maxiter,deadline):
  best_c=compute_c5
  for tau in taus:
    if time>deadline break
    EQ_N=n; target=n/2
    try:
      res=minimize(smooth_obj, h, args=(n,tau,2.0/n), method='SLSQP', bounds=[(0,1)]*n, constraints=[{'type':'eq','fun':eq_fun,'jac':eq_jac}], options={'maxiter':maxiter,'ftol':1e-12})
      new=project_feasible(res.x,n); c=compute_c5
      if c<best: ...
    except: pass
  return
```

def flip_update(c,h,p,delta,n):
   rL=n-p-1; if rL>0: ...
   lL=p; if lL>0: ...
   c[n-1] += delta*(1-2*h[p]) - delta*delta

```
def weighted_grad(h,n,w):
   wr=w[::-1]
   A=np.convolve(1.0-h, wr, mode='full')
   B=np.convolve(h, w, mode='full')
   s=n-1
   return A[s:s+n] - B[s:s+n]
```
For weights normalized length 2n-1. If n=1? not.
SLSQP polish:
```
def slsqp_polish(h,n,tau_list,iters,deadline):
   best_h = project_feasible(h,n); best_c=compute_c5
   for tau,maxit in tau_list:
      if time.time()>deadline: break
      EQ_N=n
      bounds=[(0.0,1.0)] for _ in range(n)  # list comprehension okay? no lambda
      ... constraints=[{'type':'eq','fun':eq_fun,'jac':eq_jac}]
      try:
        res=minimize(smooth_obj, best_h, args=(n,tau), method='SLSQP', bounds=bounds, constraints=constraints, options={'maxiter':maxit,'ftol':1e-12})
        new=project_feasible(res.x,n); c=compute_c5; if c<best: update
      except: pass
   return
```

Let's implement `real_pair_polish(h,n,deadline,rng,tau=10,total_iters=20000, temp=0.0)` as top-level.
Pseudo:
```
def real_pair_polish(h,n,deadline,rng,tau=10.0,max_iters=20000):
    h = np.asarray(h,dtype=float).copy()
    h = project_feasible(h,n)
    c = np.correlate(h, 1.0-h, 'full')
    cur = float(c.max())
    best_c=cur; best_h=h.copy()
    w = softmax_weights(c,tau)
    for it in range(max_iters):
        if time>deadline: break
        if it % 50 ==0: w = softmax_weights(c,tau)
        # choose p,q
        for tries in 20:
          p=int(rng.randint(n)); q=int(rng.randint(n))
          if p!=q and h[p]>1e-7 and h[q]<1-1e-7: break
        else: continue
        L = min(h[p], 1.0-h[q])
        if L <= 1e-7: continue
        A,B = pair_coeff(c,h,p,q,n)
        den = 2.0 * float(np.dot(w,B)); num = float(np.dot(w,A))
        candidates=[0.25*L,0.5*L,0.75*L,L]
        if den > 1e-14:
           tg=-num/den
           if tg > 1e-7 and tg < L-1e-7: candidates.append(tg)
        elif den < -1e-14:
           # optimum at boundary depending
           pass
        # also small gradient direction: if num<0 maybe tg positive even if den small? add 0.1L
        best_t=0.0; best_val=cur
        for t in candidates:
           if t<=0 or t>=L? include boundaries? t=0 no change; if t==L, h[p]=0 or h[q]=1.
           val=float(np.max(c + t*A + t*t*B))
           if val < best_val - 1e-12:
              best_val=val; best_t=t
        if best_t>0:
           c += best_t*A + best_t*best_t*B
           h[p] -= best_t; h[q] += best_t
           # ensure exact bounds/sum due to fp:
           if h[p] <0: h[p]=0; if h[q]>1: h[q]=1 (would have truncated t)
           cur=best_val
           if cur < best_c - 1e-12: best_c=cur; best_h=h.copy()
        else:
           # optional random small t? maybe no
    h=project_feasible(h,n); c=compute... if c<best_c update? best_h maybe.
    return best_h,best_c
```
But evaluating candidates each time from scratch with max can be heavy. We can optimize: If `cur` and best_val; okay.
Potential issue: c updated from t but due to fp, c may not exactly match correlate; after many updates drift. We can periodically recompute c = correlate(h,1-h) and project to correct errors. Do every 500 accepted or 2000 iters: `c = np.correlate(h,1.0-h,'full'); cur=c.max(); if cur != best?` This ensures accuracy. Good. Also if `h` some out due to fp, project.

`pair_coeff` uses copies; could be heavy. Can derive A,B directly via vectorized operations without 4 copies? Let's try, to speed:
For direction p->q with p decrease, q increase.
We can compute A and B arrays initialized zeros length Lc.
For p change δ=-t: linear contributions with other r (old h):
- diagonal B_p += -1 at idx n-1; A_p += (1-2h_p)
- right: for r=p+1..n-1: A[idx_pos] += - (1-h_r); A[idx_neg] += + h_r. (from δ=-t)
- left: r=0..p-1: A[idx_pos] += + h_r; A[idx_neg] += - (1-h_r)
For q change δ=+t, but h_p changed. Contributions with r≠p,q using old h:
- diagonal B_q += -1; A_q += (1-2h_q)
- right: A_pos += + (1-h_r); A_neg += - h_r
- left: A_pos += - h_r; A_neg += + (1-h_r)
For pair p-q interaction special:
Let d = |p-q|.
Case p<q (q right of p). Pair contributions final between p and q:
Original c includes two lags: offset d (p first) value h_p(1-h_q); offset -d (q first) value h_q(1-h_p).
After: p'=h_p-t, q'=h_q+t.
Value1 = p'(1-q') = (h_p-t)(1-h_q-t) = old1 - t(1-h_q) + t^2? Wait old1=h_p(1-h_q). Expand: h_p -h_q? Let's compute change: (h_p-t)(1-h_q-t)=h_p(1-h_q) - t(1-h_q) - t h_p + t^2 = old - t(1 - h_p - h_q?) no: -t[(1-h_q)+h_p] + t^2.
Value2 = q'(1-p') = (h_q+t)(1-h_p+t)=old2 + t(1-h_p)+ t h_q + t^2.
Total change for the two lags: linear = -t[(1-h_q)+h_p] + t(1-h_p) + t h_q = -t +? -(1-h_q) -h_p +1 -h_p +h_q = (-1 +h_q -h_p +1 -h_p +h_q)=2h_q -2h_p = 2(h_q - h_p). Wait plus? At t=1 derivation derivative dF/ dt for sum should g_q - g_p? The sum of both lags derivative =? For pair value sum = p(1-q)+q(1-p)=p+q-2pq. derivative (p decreases q increases): Δ=-t,+t: derivative of f = (1-2p)(-t)+ (1-2q)(t) = -1+2p +1 -2q=2(p-q). Our computed 2(h_q-h_p) sign opposite? Let's recalc value1 derivative at t: p'=p-t, q'=q+t. d(p'(1-q'))/dt = -1*(1-q') + p'*(-1)? Actually derivative: d(p-t)/dt=-1; d(1-q-t)/dt=-1; product derivative = (-1)(1-q-t) + (p-t)(-1)= -[1-q-t + p-t]= -1 +q +t -p +t = -1+q-p+2t. At t=0: q-p-1. Value2: q'=q+t,1-p'=1-p+t; derivative=(1)(1-p+t)+(q+t)(1)=1-p+t+q+t=1-p+q+2t. At t=0:1-p+q. Sum derivative = (q-p-1)+(1-p+q)=2q-2p. def pair_coeff(c,h,p,q,n):
    h1 = h.copy(); c1 = c.copy()
    apply_real_flip(c1,h1,p,-1.0,n)
    apply_real_flip(c1,h1,q,1.0,n)
    h2 = h.copy(); c2 = c.copy()
    apply_real_flip(c2,h2,p,-2.0,n)
    apply_real_flip(c2,h2,q,2.0,n)
    B = 0.5*(c2 - 2.0*c1 + c)
    A = c1 - c - B
    return A,B
```

We need ensure slice formulas for A linear include correct signs with delta. We can implement helper `add_linear_flip(A,h,p,delta,n)` exactly like flip_update but no diagonal no h update:
```
def add_linear_flip(A,h,p,delta,n):
   rL=n-p-1
   if rL>0:
      A[n:n+rL] += delta*(1-h[p+1:])
      A[n-2:p-1:-1] -= delta*h[p+1:]
   lL=p
   if lL>0:
      A[n+lL-1:n-1:-1] -= delta*h[:p]
      A[:lL] += delta*(1-h[:p])
```
This is same slice as flip_update linear. Use float h.
Then A[n-1] += delta*(1-2h[p]).
For pair p->q: add_linear_flip(A,h,p,-1,n); add_linear_flip(A,h,q,+1,n).
B sparse as above. Then c(t) = c + t A + t^2 B. Great, no copies. Much faster.

Let's define `real_pair_polish` parameters: `n_restarts?` no. Temp, max_iters, recompute every 500.

Detailed `real_pair_polish` code:
```
def real_pair_polish(h,n,max_iters,temp,tau,deadline,rng):
    h = np.asarray(h,dtype=float).copy()
    h = project_feasible(h,n)
    c = np.correlate(h,1.0-h,'full')
    cur = float(c.max())
    best_c=cur; best_h=h.copy()
    Lc=2*n-1
    A=np.zeros(Lc); B=np.zeros(Lc)
    w = softmax_weights(c,tau)
    recompute_counter=0
    for it in range(max_iters):
       if time.time()>deadline: break
       if it % 50 ==0: w=softmax_weights(c,tau)
       # choose p,q
       p=q=-1
       for _ in range(30):
          p=int(rng.randint(n)); q=int(rng.randint(n))
          if p!=q and h[p] > 1e-8 and h[q] < 1.0-1e-8: break
       if p<0: break? or continue
       limit = min(h[p], 1-h[q])
       if limit <=1e-8: continue
       A.fill(0); B.fill(0)
       add_linear_flip(A,h,p,-1.0,n)
       add_linear_flip(A,h,q,1.0,n)
       B[n-1] = -2.0
       d = abs(p-q)
       if d>0:
          B[n-1+d] += 1.0
          B[n-1-d] += 1.0
       # candidates
       den = 2.0 * float(np.dot(w, B))
       num = float(np.dot(w, A))
       cands = [0.25*limit, 0.5*limit, limit]
       if den > 1e-14:
           tg = -num/den
           if tg > 1e-9 and tg < limit-1e-9: cands.append(tg)
       elif den < -1e-14 and num > 0: # if slope positive, boundary 0 maybe; slope negative boundary L
           pass
       # maybe add tg if den not but num<0 with small? Add 0.1*limit if num < -1e-8.
       if num < -1e-9: cands.append(0.1*limit)
       best_t=0.0; best_val=cur
       # evaluate without t=0
       for t in cands:
           t2=t*t
           # compute val
           # maybe avoid full allocation by loop? use numpy max
           val = float(np.max(c + t*A + t2*B))
           if val < best_val - 1e-12:
               best_val=val; best_t=t
       if best_t > 0 and best_val <= cur + temp:
           tt=best_t*best_t
           c += best_t*A + tt*B
           h[p] -= best_t; h[q] += best_t
           # clamp caus if small
           if h[p] < 0.0: h[p]=0.0
           if h[q] > 1.0: h[q]=1.0
           cur = best_val
           if cur < best_c - 1e-12:
               best_c = cur; best_h = h.copy()
           recompute_counter += 1
       else:
           recompute_counter += 0
       every = 500
       if it % every == 499:
           h = np.clip(h,0.0,1.0)
           h = project_feasible(h,n)
           c = np.correlate(h,1.0-h,'full')
           cur = float(c.max())
           if cur < best_c - 1e-12:
               best_c = cur; best_h = h.copy()
    h = np.clip(h,0,1); h=project_feasible(h,n); c=compute...
    if c < best_c: best_h=h.copy(); best_c=c
    return best_h,best_c
```

`softmax_weights(c,tau)`:
```
def softmax_weights(c,tau):
  m=c.max(); x=(c-m)/tau; e=np.exp(x); s=e.sum(); if s<=0: return zeros with one at argmax; return e/s
```
B dot: since B sparse, dot efficient but np.dot length L. Could compute weighted A/B via precomputed active? A dense. Fine.

Candidate t from gradient: objective derivative for weighted sum `d=Σw(A+2tB)`. If B weighted positive, optimum tg. If weighted B negative (concave), minimum at boundary. If num<0 and den≤0, derivative positive? At t=0 derivative num. If num<0, decreasing initially, choose small even if den negative (function concave, minimum at boundary 0 or L). We include 0.1L if num<0. Good.
Maybe t=0.75L? Include [0.25,0.5,0.75,1.0]*limit and tg.

def polish_continuous(h,n,rng,deadline):
   h=project_feasible; c=...; best...
   # pair polish phase 1: 30% of remaining or 200s
   d1 = min(deadline, time + 0.35*(deadline-time))
   h,c=real_pair_polish(h,n,max_iters=50000,temp=1.0,tau=10,deadline=d1,rng=rng)
   h,c=real_pair_polish(h,n,max_iters=50000,temp=0.2,tau=5,deadline=min(deadline,d1+0.35*(...))) # hmm
   if time < deadline-60:
       h,c=slsqp_polish(h,n,[(5,100)],deadline-20)
   if time < deadline-20:
       h,c=exact_cd(h,n,30,deadline-2)

```

Detailed code:
```
import numpy as np
from scipy.optimize import minimize
import time

EQ_N = 0

def compute_c5(h,n): ...
def project_feasible(h,n): ...
def binary_from_scores(s,n): ...

```
def add_linear_flip(A,h,p,delta,n):
    Lr = n-p-1
    if Lr:
        A[n:n+Lr] += delta*(1.0-h[p+1:])
        A[n-2:p-1:-1] -= delta*h[p+1:]
    Lf = p
    if Lf:
        A[n+Lf-1:n-1:-1] -= delta*h[:p]
        A[:Lf] += delta*(1.0-h[:p])
    A[n-1] += delta*(1.0-2.0*h[p])
```

For binary `flip_update` if used? We decided binary local search uses `flip_update` with slices. Need same edge cases. Let's write `flip_update` for c int/float:
```
def flip_update(c,h,p,delta,n):
    Lr=n-p-1
    if Lr:
       c[n:n+Lr] += delta*(1-h[p+1:])
       if p==0:
          c[n-2::-1] -= delta*h[p+1:]
       else:
          c[n-2:p-1:-1] -= delta*h[p+1:]
    Lf=p
    if Lf:
       c[n+Lf-1:n-1:-1] -= delta*h[:p]
       c[:Lf] += delta*(1-h[:p])
    c[n-1] += delta*(1-2*h[p]) - delta*delta
```
For p=0 right negative: `c[n-2::-1]` length n-1= Lr, h[1:] length n-1. Good.

Binary c int, delta int, h int8, diagonal int. For `1-h[p+1:]` with h int8 and python 1, result int8? okay. `c` int64. For `delta*(1-h)` with delta negative, int. In-place add to int64 okay.
For diagonal: `delta*(1 - 2*h[p]) - delta*delta`; for h int8, 2*h int8; if h[p]=1, 1-2=-1 int8; delta -1 => 1; delta*delta=1; result 0. Good.

def swap_binary(c,h,i,j,n):
    hi=int(h[i]); hj=int(h[j]); delta_i=hj-hi; delta_j=hi-hj
    flip_update(c,h,i,delta_i,n); h[i]=hj
    flip_update(c,h,j,delta_j,n); h[j]=hi
```

`project_feasible`: Use diff and weights. For binary, if diff=0 skip. Need target float n/2.
```
def project_feasible(h,n):
 h=np.array(h,dtype=float)
 h=np.clip(h,0,1)
 target=n*0.5
 for _ in range(1000):
   diff=target-h.sum()
   if abs(diff)<1e-14: break
   if diff>0:
      room=1-h; tot=room.sum()
      if tot<1e-15: break
      h=np.clip(h + diff*room/tot,0,1)
   else:
      tot=h.sum()
      if tot<1e-15: break
      h=np.clip(h + diff*h/tot,0,1)
 # final tiny correction on interior
 diff=target-h.sum()
 if abs(diff)>1e-12:
    idx=int(np.argmax((h>1e-9)&(h<1-1e-9)))
    if ... h[idx]+=diff
 return h
```
But if all at boundaries and diff not zero (shouldn't for n even), might fail. Binary sum exact. Good.

```
def rand_run_binary(n,rng,max_run=6):
  h=np.zeros(n,np.int8); pos=0; bit=rng.randint(2)
  while pos<n:
    l=min(int(rng.randint(1,max_run+1)), n-pos)
    h[pos:pos+l]=bit; pos+=l; bit^=1
  h = ensure_binary_count(h,n,rng)
  return h
```

  idx -d = n-1-d = i. Same i order: c[:p] -= delta * h[:p]. (Because offset -d change -δ h_{p-d}= -δ h_i). Good.
For right j=p+d (d=1..L): values h[p+1:]

So correct `flip_update`:
```
def flip_update(c,h,p,delta,n):
    L = p
    if L:
       c[n+L-1 : n-1 : -1] += delta * (1.0 - h[:p])
       c[:L] -= delta * h[:p]
    R = n-p-1
    if R:
       c[n : n+R] -= delta * h[p+1:]
       if p == 0:
          c[n-2 :: -1] += delta * (1.0 - h[1:])
       else:
          c[n-2 : p-1 : -1] += delta * (1.0 - h[p+1:])
    c[n-1] += delta * (1.0 - 2.0*h[p]) - delta*delta
```
For binary h int8, `1.0 - h` float? For int c, cast issue. We can use `1 - h` (int) not 1.0 to keep int. But for real A, float. Separate functions? We can write `flip_update` using multiplication with delta and expressions; if c int and h int, `1 - h` int, delta int -> int. Use `1 - h` for binary. For real, use same with float h and c, works with `1.0 - h`? We can use `1.0 - ...` but for int c in-place float cast error. We can split: `flip_update_int` for binary, `add_linear_flip_float` for real. Or make `flip_update` generic using `one = np.float64 if np.issubdtype(h.dtype,np.floating) else 1`? Simpler implement two functions? `flip_update` for binary with int, and `add_linear_flip` for float with correct formulas. Good.

Binary local uses `flip_update` int. Real pair uses `add_linear_flip` float with linear part only (no diagonal quadratic):
```
def add_linear_flip(A,h,p,delta,n):
    L=p; if L:
       A[n+L-1:n-1:-1] += delta*(1.0-h[:p])
       A[:L] -= delta*h[:p]
    R=n-p-1; if R:
       A[n:n+R] -= delta*h[p+1:]
       if p==0: A[n-2::-1] += delta*(1.0-h[1:])
       else: A[n-2:p-1:-1] += delta*(1.0-h[p+1:])
    A[n-1] += delta*(1.0-2.0*h[p])
```
Correct.

```
def add_linear_flip(A,h,p,delta,n):
    L=p
    if L:
        A[n+L-1:n-1:-1] += delta*(1.0 - h[:p])
        A[n-L-1:n-1] -= delta*h[:p]
    R=n-p-1
    if R:
        A[n:n+R] -= delta*h[p+1:]
        if p == 0:
            A[n-2::-1] += delta*(1.0 - h[1:])
        else:
            A[n-2:p-1:-1] += delta*(1.0 - h[p+1:])
    A[n-1] += delta*(1.0 - 2.0*h[p])
```
Check left complement slice `A[n-L-1:n-1]`: length L? start n-L-1, stop n-1 exclusive => indices n-L-1 .. n-2 length L. For L=1,n=3 start1 stop2 idx1. Good. For p=n-1, start0 stop n-1 length n-1. For p=0 skip.

```
def flip_update(c,h,p,delta,n):
    L=p
    if L:
       c[n+L-1:n-1:-1] += delta*(1 - h[:p])
       c[n-L-1:n-1] -= delta*h[:p]
    R=n-p-1
    if R:
       c[n:n+R] -= delta*h[p+1:]
       if p==0:
          c[n-2::-1] += delta*(1 - h[1:])
       else:
          c[n-2:p-1:-1] += delta*(1 - h[p+1:])
    c[n-1] += delta*(1 - 2*h[p]) - delta*delta
```
For h int8, `1 - h` int8, delta int; c int64. Good.

```python
import numpy as np
import time


def compute_c5(h, n):
    return float(np.max(np.correlate(h, 1.0 - h, mode="full")) * (2.0 / n))


def project_feasible(h, n):
    h = np.array(h, dtype=float).copy()
    h = np.clip(h, 0.0, 1.0)
    target = 0.5 * n
    for _ in range(60):
        diff = target - float(h.sum())
        if abs(diff) < 1e-14:
            break
        if diff > 0:
            room = 1.0 - h
            tot = float(room.sum())
            if tot < 1e-15:
                break
            h = np.clip(h + (diff / tot) * room, 0.0, 1.0)
        else:
            tot = float(h.sum())
            if tot < 1e-15:
                break
            h = np.clip(h + (diff / tot) * h, 0.0, 1.0)
    diff = target - float(h.sum())
    if abs(diff) > 1e-12:
        m = (h > 1e-12) & (h < 1.0 - 1e-12)
        if m.any():
            h[m] += diff / float(m.sum())
            h = np.clip(h, 0.0, 1.0)
    return h


def binary_from_scores(scores, n):
    s = np.asarray(scores, dtype=float)
    if s.size != n:
        xo = np.linspace(0.0, 2.0, s.size, endpoint=False)
        xn = np.linspace(0.0, 2.0, n, endpoint=False)
        s = np.interp(xn, xo, s)
    order = np.argsort(s)[::-1]
    out = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.int8)
    out[order[:n // 2]] = 1
    return out


def flip_update(c, h, p, delta, n):
    L = p
    if L:
        c[n + L - 1:n - 1:-1] += delta * (1 - h[:p])
        c[n - L - 1:n - 1] -= delta * h[:p]
    R = n - p - 1
    if R:
        c[n:n + R] -= delta * h[p + 1:]
        if p == 0:
            c[n - 2::-1] += delta * (1 - h[1:])
        else:
            c[n - 2:p - 1:-1] += delta * (1 - h[p + 1:])
    c[n - 1] += delta * (1 - 2 * h[p]) - delta * delta


def swap_binary(c, h, i, j, n):
    hi = int(h[i])
    hj = int(h[j])
    di = hj - hi
    dj = hi - hj
    flip_update(c, h, i, di, n)
    h[i] = hj
    flip_update(c, h, j, dj, n)
    h[j] = hi


def binary_local_search(h, n, deadline, rng, target_c):
    h = np.array(h, dtype=np.int8).copy()
    h64 = h.astype(np.int64)
    c = np.correlate(h64, 1 - h64, mode="full").astype(np.int64)
    cur = int(c.max())
    best = cur
    best_h = h.copy()

    ones_list = np.array(np.flatnonzero(h), dtype=int)
    zeros_list = np.array(np.flatnonzero(1 - h), dtype=int)
    pos1 = np.full(n, -1, dtype=int)
    pos0 = np.full(n, -1, dtype=int)
    for k, idx in enumerate(ones_list):
        pos1[idx] = k
    for k, idx in enumerate(zeros_list):
        pos0[idx] = k

    target_count = int(np.floor(target_c * n / 2.0))
    t0 = time.time()
    allotted = max(0.0, deadline - t0)
    phases = ((2, 1.0, 0.18), (1, 0.65, 0.45), (0, 0.25, 0.37))

    for allow, eq, frac in phases:
        end = min(deadline, t0 + allotted * frac)
        no_improve = 0
        top_lags = np.argpartition(c, -min(5, c.size))[-min(5, c.size):]
        while time.time() < end:
            if not ones_list.size or not zeros_list.size:
                break
            i = j = -1
            oi = oz = -1
            if rng.random() < 0.45 and cur > target_count:
                idx = int(top_lags[int(rng.randint(len(top_lags)))])
                k = idx - (n - 1)
                arr = None
                if k > 0:
                    arr = np.flatnonzero((h[k:] == 1) & (h[:-k] == 0)) + k
                elif k < 0:
                    d = -k
                    arr = np.flatnonzero((h[:n - d] == 1) & (h[d:] == 0))
                if arr is not None and arr.size:
                    i = int(arr[int(rng.randint(arr.size))])
                    oi = int(pos1[i])
                oz = int(rng.randint(len(zeros_list)))
                j = int(zeros_list[oz])
            if oi < 0:
                oi = int(rng.randint(len(ones_list)))
                i = int(ones_list[oi])
                if oz < 0:
                    oz = int(rng.randint(len(zeros_list)))
                    j = int(zeros_list[oz])

            swap_binary(c, h, i, j, n)
            new = int(c.max())

            accept = False
            if new < cur:
                accept = True
            elif new == cur:
                accept = rng.random() < eq
            elif allow > 0 and new <= cur + allow:
                accept = True

            if accept:
                cur = new
                ones_list[oi] = j
                zeros_list[oz] = i
                pos1[j] = oi
                pos0[i] = oz
                pos1[i] = -1
                pos0[j] = -1
                if cur < best:
                    best = cur
                    best_h = h.copy()
                    no_improve = 0
                else:
                    no_improve += 1
            else:
                swap_binary(c, h, i, j, n)
                no_improve += 1

            if allow == 0 and no_improve > 40000:
                break
            if no_improve % 80 == 0:
                top_lags = np.argpartition(c, -min(5, c.size))[-min(5, c.size):]
        if allow == 0:
            break

    b64 = best_h.astype(np.int64)
    best = int(np.max(np.correlate(b64, 1 - b64, mode="full")))
    return best_h, best


def softmax_weights(c, tau):
    m = float(c.max())
    e = np.exp((c - m) / tau)
    s = float(e.sum())
    if s <= 1e-300:
        w = np.zeros(c.size, dtype=float)
        w[int(np.argmax(c))] = 1.0
        return w
    return e / s


def add_linear_flip(A, h, p, delta, n):
    L = p
    if L:
        A[n + L - 1:n - 1:-1] += delta * (1.0 - h[:p])
        A[n - L - 1:n - 1] -= delta * h[:p]
    R = n - p - 1
    if R:
        A[n:n + R] -= delta * h[p + 1:]
        if p == 0:
            A[n - 2::-1] += delta * (1.0 - h[1:])
        else:
            A[n - 2:p - 1:-1] += delta * (1.0 - h[p + 1:])
    A[n - 1] += delta * (1.0 - 2.0 * h[p])


def real_pair_polish(h, n, max_iters, temp, tau, deadline, rng):
    h = np.array(h, dtype=float).copy()
    h = project_feasible(h, n)
    c = np.correlate(h, 1.0 - h, mode="full")
    cur = float(c.max())
    best_c = cur
    best_h = h.copy()

    A = np.zeros(2 * n - 1, dtype=float)
    B = np.zeros(2 * n - 1, dtype=float)
    w = softmax_weights(c, tau)
    fine = temp <= 1e-9
    fracs = (0.05, 0.10, 0.20, 0.30, 0.40, 0.50, 0.60, 0.70, 0.80, 0.90, 1.00) if fine else (0.25, 0.50, 0.75, 1.00)

    for it in range(max_iters):
        if time.time() > deadline:
            break
        if it % 50 == 0:
            w = softmax_weights(c, tau)

        p = -1
        for _ in range(50):
            p = int(rng.randint(n))
            q = int(rng.randint(n))
            if p != q and h[p] > 1e-8 and h[q] < 1.0 - 1e-8:
                break
        if p < 0:
            break

        lim = min(h[p], 1.0 - h[q])
        if lim <= 1e-8:
            continue

        A.fill(0.0)
        B.fill(0.0)
        add_linear_flip(A, h, p, -1.0, n)
        add_linear_flip(A, h, q, 1.0, n)
        B[n - 1] = -2.0
        d = abs(p - q)
        if d:
            B[n - 1 + d] += 1.0
            B[n - 1 - d] += 1.0

        den = 2.0 * float(np.dot(w, B))
        num = float(np.dot(w, A))
        best_t = 0.0
        best_val = float("inf")

        for f in fracs:
            t = f * lim
            if t <= 1e-12:
                continue
            val = float(np.max(c + t * A + (t * t) * B))
            if val < best_val:
                best_val = val
                best_t = t

        if abs(den) > 1e-14:
            tg = -num / den
            if 1e-10 < tg < lim - 1e-10:
                val = float(np.max(c + tg * A + (tg * tg) * B))
                if val < best_val:
                    best_val = val
                    best_t = tg

        if best_t > 0.0 and best_val <= cur + temp + 1e-12:
            tt = best_t * best_t
            c += best_t * A + tt * B
            h[p] -= best_t
            h[q] += best_t
            if h[p] < 0.0:
                h[p] = 0.0
            if h[q] > 1.0:
                h[q] = 1.0
            cur = best_val
            if cur < best_c - 1e-13:
                best_c = cur
                best_h = h.copy()

        if it % 450 == 449:
            h = np.clip(h, 0.0, 1.0)
            h = project_feasible(h, n)
            c = np.correlate(h, 1.0 - h, mode="full")
            cur = float(c.max())
            if cur < best_c - 1e-13:
                best_c = cur
                best_h = h.copy()

    h = np.clip(h, 0.0, 1.0)
    h = project_feasible(h, n)
    c_cur = float(np.max(np.correlate(h, 1.0 - h, mode="full")) * (2.0 / n))
    c_best = float(np.max(np.correlate(best_h, 1.0 - best_h, mode="full")) * (2.0 / n))
    if c_cur <= c_best:
        best_h = h.copy()
        best_c = c_cur
    else:
        best_c = c_best
    return best_h, best_c


def polish_continuous(h, n, rng, deadline):
    h = np.array(h, dtype=float).copy()
    h = project_feasible(h, n)
    best_h = h.copy()
    best_c = compute_c5(h, n)
    rem = deadline - time.time()
    if rem < 12.0:
        return best_h, best_c

    d1 = min(deadline, time.time() + 0.30 * rem)
    if d1 > time.time() + 6.0:
        h1, c1 = real_pair_polish(h, n, 100000, 1.0, 8.0, d1, rng)
        if c1 < best_c:
            best_h, best_c = h1, c1

    rem = deadline - time.time()
    if rem < 12.0:
        return best_h, best_c
    d2 = min(deadline, time.time() + 0.35 * rem)
    if d2 > time.time() + 6.0:
        h2, c2 = real_pair_polish(best_h, n, 100000, 0.08, 4.0, d2, rng)
        if c2 < best_c:
            best_h, best_c = h2, c2

    rem = deadline - time.time()
    if rem > 12.0:
        h3, c3 = real_pair_polish(best_h, n, 100000, 0.0, 1.6, deadline, rng)
        if c3 < best_c:
            best_h, best_c = h3, c3

    best_h = project_feasible(best_h, n)
    best_c = compute_c5(best_h, n)
    return best_h, best_c


def run(seed=42, budget_s=1100, **kwargs):
    t0 = time.time()
    deadline = t0 + budget_s - 3.0
    rng = np.random.RandomState(seed)

    try:
        h_best = np.array(initial_h_values, dtype=float).copy()
        n_best = len(h_best)
    except Exception:
        n_best = 500
        h_best = np.full(n_best, 0.5)

    h_best = project_feasible(h_best, n_best)
    best_c = compute_c5(h_best, n_best)
    candidates = [(h_best.copy(), best_c, n_best)]

    binary_deadline = max(t0 + 20.0, min(deadline, t0 + min(700.0, 0.55 * budget_s)))
    slot = max(20.0, min(300.0, 0.45 * (binary_deadline - t0)))
    parent_deadline = min(binary_deadline, t0 + slot)
    if n_best % 2 == 0 and time.time() < parent_deadline - 5.0:
        hb = binary_from_scores(h_best, n_best)
        hb, cb = binary_local_search(hb, n_best, parent_deadline, rng, 0.3808)
        cb5 = cb * (2.0 / n_best)
        if cb5 < best_c:
            best_c = cb5
            best_h = hb.astype(float)
            n_best = n_best
        candidates.append((hb.astype(float), cb5, n_best))

    others = [650, 600, 550, 750, 800]
    for nt in others:
        if time.time() > binary_deadline - 35.0:
            break
        if nt == n_best:
            continue
        slot = min(90.0, max(25.0, 0.20 * max(0.0, binary_deadline - time.time())))
        d = min(binary_deadline, time.time() + slot)
        hb = binary_from_scores(best_h, nt)
        hb, cb = binary_local_search(hb, nt, d, rng, 0.3808)
        cb5 = cb * (2.0 / nt)
        candidates.append((hb.astype(float), cb5, nt))
        if cb5 < best_c:
            best_c = cb5
            h_best = hb.astype(float)
            n_best = nt

    candidates.sort(key=lambda x: x[1])
    polished = 0
    for ch, cc, nn in candidates:
        if polished >= 2:
            break
        if time.time() > deadline - 40.0:
            break
        slot = min(280.0, max(45.0, 0.42 * max(0.0, deadline - time.time())))
        d = min(deadline, time.time() + slot)
        hpol, cpol = polish_continuous(ch, nn, rng, d)
        candidates[polished] = (hpol, cpol, nn)
        if cpol < best_c:
            best_c = cpol
            h_best = hpol.copy()
            n_best = nn
        polished += 1

    if time.time() < deadline - 8.0:
        final_deadline = deadline - 2.0
        h_best, best_c = real_pair_polish(h_best, n_best, 50000, 0.0, 1.0, final_deadline, rng)

    h_best = project_feasible(h_best, n_best)
    best_c = compute_c5(h_best, n_best)
    return h_best, best_c, int(n_best)
```
